## Claude tool result: toolu_01SYHb6q8ffFCdzMxb94zRDk · 2026-08-27T16:10:32.004Z

```text
1	/**
2	 * Single source of truth for the unified worker conversation stream on
3	 * the frontend.
4	 *
5	 * Per (workerId) we hold a contiguous *window* [lowestSeq..latestContiguousSeq]:
6	 *   - entries: the contiguous window in seq order, never with gaps.
7	 *   - lowestSeq: the lowest seq currently in `entries` (0 if empty).
8	 *   - latestContiguousSeq: the highest seq we have proven contiguous
9	 *     from lowestSeq. Used as the cursor for forward refetch.
10	 *   - latestKnownSeq: the highest seq the server has told us exists.
11	 *     Updates immediately on a wake-up frame; the fetch fills the gap.
12	 *   - hasOlder: true if older entries exist on the server before
13	 *     lowestSeq. Set by initial tail-load and `loadOlder`.
14	 *   - status: "idle" → "loading" → "loaded"; "error" on a fetch failure.
15	 *
16	 * Initial load fetches the last N entries via `?limit=N`. Forward
17	 * extension uses `?afterSeq=latestContiguousSeq`. Backward hydration
18	 * uses `?beforeSeq=lowestSeq&limit=N` via `loadOlder`.
19	 *
20	 * SSE frames carry only `{ workerId, seq }` — the content is fetched
21	 * via the HTTP endpoint. The frame is a wake-up hint; we never trust
22	 * it as the entry payload.
23	 */
24	import { useCallback, useEffect, useMemo, useSyncExternalStore } from "react";
25	import { coalesceWorkerEntriesById, type WorkerEntry } from "@/shared/worker-entries";
26	import type { RuntimeAPIs } from "@/runtime-api/types";
27	import { useRuntimeAPIs } from "@/runtime-api/provider";
28	
29	/**
30	 * Collapse multiple rows that share an entry `id` into a single
31	 * conversation item. The server's unified stream intentionally appends
32	 * a new row whenever an entry's text grows (so a streaming assistant
33	 * message can be revised in place — see the
34	 * `appends changed bridge message revisions so streaming prose can expand`
35	 * test in `tests/server/workers/output-store.test.ts`). It also
36	 * writes the same user_input id onto a newer worker's stream when a run
37	 * cycles through workers.
38	 *
39	 * Coalesce policy:
40	 *   - Keep the entry at its FIRST appearance in the input order, so
41	 *     conversational position doesn't jump when text grows.
42	 *   - Use the LATEST revision's text / metadata (text grows; latest
43	 *     revision is the finished one).
44	 *   - Take the timestamp from the copy on the NEWEST worker, because that
45	 *     is the one the live thread actually contains. Neither "earliest" nor
46	 *     "latest" works on its own: a rewind re-delivers a message with a new
47	 *     timestamp (newest is right, earliest pins it back above output it was
48	 *     rewound past), while `reconcileWorkerUserMessagesInStream` backfills
49	 *     history onto a fresh worker with the original `createdAt` (earliest is
50	 *     right, latest drags the opening prompt into the middle of the day).
51	 *     Worker order settles both. Copies from the same worker — streaming
52	 *     revisions, which share a timestamp anyway — keep the earliest.
53	 *   - Entries without an id pass through unchanged.
54	 *
55	 * `workerOrder` is the run's workers in creation order. Without it (a single
56	 * worker stream, or a caller that has no worker list yet) the earliest
57	 * timestamp is kept, which is the conservative choice: a message stays where
58	 * it already is.
59	 */
60	export { coalesceWorkerEntriesById };
61	
62	type WorkerEntryStorage = Pick<Storage, "getItem" | "setItem" | "removeItem">;
63	
64	// v2: transcript recovery renumbered seqs and changed entry ids on repaired
65	// worker streams. This cache merges with server data by entry id, so a v1
66	// cache keeps rendering stale pre-repair entries (raw streaming fragments)
67	// alongside the fixed ones — a transcript shredded mid-word. Bumping the key
68	// retires those caches instead of requiring users to clear storage by hand.
69	const DEFAULT_STORAGE_KEY = "omni-worker-entries-cache:v2";
70	const DEFAULT_MAX_WORKERS = 16;
71	const DEFAULT_MAX_SERIALIZED_BYTES = 4_000_000;
72	const DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT = 100;
73	const DEFAULT_OLDER_LIMIT = 100;
74	// Entries are remembered on every append and every stream poll. Serializing
75	// the whole cache envelope synchronously on each one is main-thread work that
76	// competes with keystrokes, so the write is coalesced onto a trailing timer
77	// while reads stay served from the in-memory envelope.
78	const DEFAULT_FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS = 1_000;
79	
80	interface CachedWorkerStream {
81	  updatedAt: number;
82	  entries: WorkerEntry[];
83	}
84	
85	interface WorkerEntriesCacheEnvelope {
86	  version: 1;
87	  workers: Record<string, CachedWorkerStream>;
88	}
89	
90	export type WorkerStreamStatus = "idle" | "loading" | "loaded" | "error";
91	
92	export interface WorkerStreamState {
93	  workerId: string;
94	  entries: WorkerEntry[];
95	  lowestSeq: number;
96	  latestContiguousSeq: number;
97	  latestKnownSeq: number;
98	  hasOlder: boolean;
99	  status: WorkerStreamStatus;
100	  lastError: string | null;
101	  needsTailValidation?: boolean;
102	}
103	
104	type Listener = (state: WorkerStreamState) => void;
105	
106	interface FetchResponse {
107	  entries: WorkerEntry[];
108	  latestSeq: number;
109	  hasOlder?: boolean;
110	}
111	
112	export interface WorkerEntriesManagerOptions {
113	  listEntries?: RuntimeAPIs["workers"]["listEntries"];
114	  storage?: WorkerEntryStorage | null;
115	  storageKey?: string;
116	  maxWorkers?: number;
117	  maxSerializedBytes?: number;
118	  now?: () => number;
119	  flushIntervalMs?: number;
120	}
121	
122	function latestSeq(entries: WorkerEntry[]) {
123	  return entries.reduce((latest, entry) => (
124	    typeof entry.seq === "number" && Number.isFinite(entry.seq)
125	      ? Math.max(latest, entry.seq)
126	      : latest
127	  ), 0);
128	}
129	
130	function initialState(
131	  workerId: string,
132	  entries: WorkerEntry[] = [],
133	  options: { needsTailValidation?: boolean } = {},
134	): WorkerStreamState {
135	  const latest = latestSeq(entries);
136	  const lowest = entries.length > 0
137	    ? entries.reduce((acc, entry) => (
138	      typeof entry.seq === "number" && entry.seq > 0 && (acc === 0 || entry.seq < acc) ? entry.seq : acc
139	    ), 0)
140	    : 0;
141	  return {
142	    workerId,
143	    entries,
144	    lowestSeq: lowest,
145	    latestContiguousSeq: latest,
146	    latestKnownSeq: latest,
147	    hasOlder: lowest > 1,
148	    status: entries.length > 0 ? "loaded" : "idle",
149	    lastError: null,
150	    needsTailValidation: options.needsTailValidation,
151	  };
152	}
153	
154	function defaultStorage(): WorkerEntryStorage | null {
155	  // Worker transcript bodies are server-owned and fetched through the
156	  // unified worker stream. Persisting them in localStorage can crowd out
157	  // preferences and credentials, so browser storage is opt-in for tests or
158	  // explicitly bounded consumers only.
159	  return null;
160	}
161	
162	function emptyEnvelope(): WorkerEntriesCacheEnvelope {
163	  return {
164	    version: 1,
165	    workers: {},
166	  };
167	}
168	
169	function isWorkerEntryArray(value: unknown): value is WorkerEntry[] {
170	  return Array.isArray(value) && value.every((entry) => (
171	    typeof entry === "object"
172	    && entry !== null
173	    && typeof (entry as Partial<WorkerEntry>).id === "string"
174	    && typeof (entry as Partial<WorkerEntry>).seq === "number"
175	  ));
176	}
177	
178	function compactEntryForCache(entry: WorkerEntry): WorkerEntry {
179	  return {
180	    ...entry,
181	    raw: undefined,
182	  };
183	}
184	
185	export const EMPTY_WORKER_STREAM_STATE: WorkerStreamState = initialState("__none__");
186	
187	export class WorkerEntriesManager {
188	  private readonly stateByWorker = new Map<string, WorkerStreamState>();
189	  private readonly listenersByWorker = new Map<string, Set<Listener>>();
190	  private readonly inFlightByWorker = new Map<string, Promise<void>>();
191	  private readonly olderLoadInFlightByWorker = new Set<string>();
192	  private readonly wakeVersionByWorker = new Map<string, number>();
193	  private readonly everLoadedWorkers = new Set<string>();
194	  private listEntries: RuntimeAPIs["workers"]["listEntries"] | null;
195	  private readonly storage: WorkerEntryStorage | null;
196	  private readonly storageKey: string;
197	  private readonly maxWorkers: number;
198	  private readonly maxSerializedBytes: number;
199	  private readonly now: () => number;
200	  private readonly flushIntervalMs: number;
201	  private envelope: WorkerEntriesCacheEnvelope | null = null;
202	  private flushTimer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;
203	
204	  constructor(options: WorkerEntriesManagerOptions = {}) {
205	    const usesDefaultStorage = options.storage === undefined;
206	    this.listEntries = options.listEntries ?? null;
207	    this.storage = usesDefaultStorage ? defaultStorage() : options.storage ?? null;
208	    this.storageKey = options.storageKey ?? DEFAULT_STORAGE_KEY;
209	    this.maxWorkers = options.maxWorkers ?? DEFAULT_MAX_WORKERS;
210	    this.maxSerializedBytes = options.maxSerializedBytes ?? DEFAULT_MAX_SERIALIZED_BYTES;
211	    this.now = options.now ?? Date.now;
212	    this.flushIntervalMs = options.flushIntervalMs ?? DEFAULT_FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS;
213	
214	    if (usesDefaultStorage && this.storage && typeof window !== "undefined") {
215	      const flushNow = () => this.flushCache();
216	      window.addEventListener("pagehide", flushNow);
217	      window.addEventListener("visibilitychange", flushNow);
218	    }
219	  }
220	
221	  configure(listEntries: RuntimeAPIs["workers"]["listEntries"]) {
222	    this.listEntries = listEntries;
223	  }
224	
225	  private load(input: Parameters<RuntimeAPIs["workers"]["listEntries"]>[0]) {
226	    if (!this.listEntries) {
227	      return Promise.reject(new Error("Worker entries are not connected to the runtime."));
228	    }
229	    return this.listEntries(input) as Promise<FetchResponse>;
230	  }

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-27T16:10:49.708Z

With both managers understood, I'm now designing a shared retention policy module with a hard cap on retained entries and an idle-time threshold for pruning stale, unaccessed memory entries.

The pruning logic keeps a hot window of recent entries always in memory, extends retention if the user recently accessed scrollback, and otherwise collapses back down to the hot window while hard-capping total entries — flagging trimmed state and updating the lowest sequence marker accordingly. Now I'm working through a tricky wrinkle: for ConversationTranscriptManager, trimming from the head invalidates the `oldestToken` used for pagination, so I need to figure out whether entries carry their own tokens to reconstruct a valid oldest token after trimming.

I'm figuring out where `lastScrollbackAccessAt` should be set — `loadOlder()` seems like the natural trigger since it signals the user scrolled to the top, and marking access there is simplest. I'm also deciding to prune on append rather than on a timer, since idle sessions aren't growing anyway. Next I want to look at `buildAgentOutputActivity` for memo identity design, check Terminal.tsx around the useMemo for visibleEntries, and inspect how the server derives transcript tokens — so I'll read through those sections next.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-27T16:16:30.629Z

I'm figuring out what question to ask next, since I've been digging through the code to design the retention garbage collector. The user already settled on pruning old, unaccessed data from memory, so now I need to nail down the actual policy details—what counts as "accessed" and what age thresholds to use.

I noticed the transcript manager relies on opaque token-based pagination, which means trimming from the head requires tracking an oldest token, but I couldn't locate the right server file to confirm this—that's a technical detail I should sort out myself rather than surface as a question. The real things worth asking about are the retention thresholds (hot window, max retained, idle age) and how to define "accessed" (just scrollback loads, or any session view). Whether pruning runs only on append versus also on a timer feels more like an implementation choice I can decide on my own—append-only pruning seems reasonable.

I want to give concrete numeric options for the retention policy since sessions could easily accumulate 20k+ entries over a day. A moderate approach might keep 300 entries as a hot window and up to 2000 if scrollback was touched in the last 10 minutes, dropping back down otherwise; an aggressive option would use smaller numbers with a shorter idle window for lower memory use at the cost of more refetching; and a generous option would keep much larger windows with a longer idle threshold. For the access signal, I'm weighing whether "accessed" should only count explicit scrollback actions like loading older entries or scrolling near the top.

I also want to add a third question about background sessions—whether non-visible sessions/workers should be pruned more aggressively than the focused one, since OpenHands likely keeps many workers in memory simultaneously, and pruning inactive ones down to the hot window immediately could be a significant memory win. I'll present all three questions with recommended defaults.

{
  "questions": [
    {
      "question": "How aggressive should the retention budget be? (entries kept per worker stream in memory)",
      "header": "Budget",
      "multiSelect": false,
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Hot 300 / max 2000 / idle 10min (Recommended)",
          "description": "Always keep the newest 300 entries. If you touched scrollback in the last 10 minutes, keep up to 2000. Otherwise collapse back to 300. A day-long session settles around 300 rows instead of tens of thousands, and reading back through history still feels instant."
        },
        {
          "label": "Hot 150 / max 600 / idle 5min",
          "description": "Tighter. Lowest memory and DOM, smoothest streaming, but scrolling back more than a few screens triggers a refetch more often."
        },
        {
          "label": "Hot 500 / max 5000 / idle 30min",
          "description": "Generous. Long scrollback stays resident, at the cost of a much bigger DOM. Probably still slow on the exact session you described."
        }
      ]
    },
    {
      "question": "What should reset an entry's \"recently accessed\" clock?",
      "header": "Access",
      "multiSelect": false,
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Only scrollback (Recommended)",
          "description": "The clock resets when you actually scroll toward the top / a loadOlder fires. Watching live output at the bottom does not count as accessing old history, so a session you leave streaming for a day prunes itself down."
        },
        {
          "label": "Any activity in the session",
          "description": "Scrolling anywhere, focusing the session, or new output all reset the clock. An actively-watched session then never prunes below the max, which mostly defeats the point for your day-long case."
        }
      ]
    },
    {
      "question": "Should streams that aren't on screen be pruned harder?",
      "header": "Background",
      "multiSelect": false,
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Yes, collapse to hot window (Recommended)",
          "description": "When a worker stream stops being rendered, drop it straight to the hot window without waiting out the idle timer. Switching between conversations over a long day is probably a large share of the retained memory."
        },
        {
          "label": "No, one policy everywhere",
          "description": "Background streams age out on the same timer as the visible one. Simpler and easier to reason about, but memory from previously-visited sessions lingers."
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}

      "header": "Budget",
      "multiSelect": false,
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Hot 300 / max 2000 / idle 10min (Recommended)",
          "description": "Always keep the newest 300 entries. If you touched scrollback in the last 10 minutes, keep up to 2000. Otherwise collapse back to 300. A day-long session settles around 300 rows instead of tens of thousands, and reading back through history still feels instant."
        },
        {
          "label": "Hot 150 / max 600 / idle 5min",
          "description": "Tighter. Lowest memory and DOM, smoothest streaming, but scrolling back more than a few screens triggers a refetch more often."
        },
        {
          "label": "Hot 500 / max 5000 / idle 30min",
          "description": "Generous. Long scrollback stays resident, at the cost of a much bigger DOM. Probably still slow on the exact session you described."
        }
      ]
    },
    {
      "question": "What should reset an entry's \"recently accessed\" clock?",
      "header": "Access",
      "multiSelect": false,
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Only scrollback (Recommended)",
          "description": "The clock resets when you actually scroll toward the top or a loadOlder fires. Watching live output at the bottom does not count as accessing old history, so a session left streaming for a day prunes itself down."
        },
        {
          "label": "Any activity in the session",
          "description": "Scrolling anywhere, focusing the session, or new output all reset the clock. An actively-watched session then never prunes below the max, which mostly defeats the point for your day-long case."
        }
      ]
    },
    {
      "question": "Should streams that aren't on screen be pruned harder?",
      "header": "Background",
      "multiSelect": false,
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Yes, collapse to hot window (Recommended)",
          "description": "When a worker stream stops being rendered, drop it straight to the hot window without waiting out the idle timer. Switching between conversations over a long day is probably a large share of the retained memory."
        },
        {
          "label": "No, one policy everywhere",
          "description": "Background streams age out on the same timer as the visible one. Simpler and easier to reason about, but memory from previously-visited sessions lingers."
        }
      ]

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-29T18:17:02.620Z

I've settled on the full retention spec: a hot window of 300 entries always kept, a ceiling of 2000 entries when scrollback was recently accessed, a 10-minute idle threshold, with the access clock only resetting on loadOlder calls, and background streams collapsing immediately to the hot window when not actively subscribed.

The scope covers capping the live window in WorkerEntriesManager and ConversationTranscriptManager, plus fixing memo identity in buildAgentOutputActivity so unchanged entries produce identical object references. My plan: resolve whether trimming the transcript head breaks oldestToken, write the retention module, wire it into both managers, fix the identity issue, add tests, and build.

I want to start by checking the server transcript route and existing tests for these managers. The memo identity fix seems like the trickier piece — I'm thinking through caching the previous entries-to-items mapping so buildAgentOutputActivity can reuse object references instead of rebuilding fresh each call.

Since items mutate in place as new entries arrive (thoughts appended, tool updates, protocol replacement), a late entry can retroactively change an earlier item, so true incremental building isn't safe. A better approach is to build the full output as before, then reconcile it against the previous result by id/key, reusing previous object references wherever the new item is structurally equal. This comparison is much cheaper than a React re-render, and since the window is capped at a small size, the rebuild cost stays bounded. So the fix becomes a `reconcileActivityIdentity(previous, next)` pass that substitutes matching previous references into `next`, letting memoized ActivityRow components skip unnecessary re-renders.

I'm now working out where to store `previous` — since the reconcile function is exported and reused elsewhere, a caller-side cache (like a ref inside Terminal's useMemo) fits the codebase's preference against hidden global state better than a module-level cache. That means keeping a `previousActivityRef` in Terminal and running the reconciliation after the full activity array (merged and sorted) is built, even though mutating a ref inside useMemo is slightly impure — it's an acceptable pattern for identity caching given the project avoids useEffect for state updates. I also need to check whether `groupConsecutiveTools` creates new group wrapper objects each time, since that would need reconciling too.

For comparing items, I'm leaning toward a targeted structural comparison instead of a fully generic deep-equal, to keep things fast and predictable — though a generic deep-equal limited to plain values is simpler. The tricky part is that `raw` on protocol items can be large and arbitrary, so deep-comparing it every rebuild could be costly; instead I'll compare `raw` by reference first, since entries come from a stable manager and their `raw` reference should stay stable across rebuilds, falling back to non-reuse only when references differ.

Now I want to check the existing tests and the server transcript before writing the code, so I'll start exploring both in parallel.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-29T18:17:17.219Z

I'm figuring out how to derive a new pagination token client-side by computing, for each worker, the sequence number just before the oldest entry being retained, so that trimming the head still returns entries correctly when queried with `beforeToken`.

Reconstructing the token encoding on the client would duplicate logic that currently lives server-side in the route module. The cleaner fix is to move the token codec into a shared module both sides can import, avoiding a parallel implementation — I want to check the route's `beforeToken` handling to confirm the exact filtering semantics before doing that.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-29T18:17:31.897Z

I'm noting that the token codec relies on Node's Buffer, which isn't available client-side, so I'd need a cross-platform base64url helper if moving this to shared code. I'm also questioning whether trimming the transcript head is actually necessary for the user's long-running direct-control session, and checking which manager handles that stream at the relevant line.

Trying to keep `oldestToken` unchanged while trimming the head seems wrong—it would leave a gap in the entries `loadOlder` could fetch, breaking scrollback. The cleanest solution is to track a client-side trim marker: when trimming, record cursors for the new oldest retained entry and set `oldestToken` to a locally-encoded token, moving the codec into a shared platform-neutral module used by both the route and the manager, consistent with the project's rules against parallel layers.

Now I want to check whether base64 helpers already exist in shared, then read through the rest of WorkerEntriesManager—loadOlder, rememberState, and the subscribe/unsubscribe logic for background pruning—before making the edits.

284	  hasEverLoaded(workerId: string): boolean {
285	    if (this.everLoadedWorkers.has(workerId)) {

287	    }

289	    if (state && state.status === "loaded" && !state.needsTailValidation) {

293	    return false;

295	
296	  subscribe(workerId: string, listener: Listener): () => void {
297	    let bucket = this.listenersByWorker.get(workerId);
298	    if (!bucket) {
299	      bucket = new Set<Listener>();
300	      this.listenersByWorker.set(workerId, bucket);
301	    }
302	    bucket.add(listener);
303	    return () => {
304	      bucket?.delete(listener);
305	      if (bucket && bucket.size === 0) {
306	        this.listenersByWorker.delete(workerId);
307	      }
308	    };
309	  }
310	
311	  ensureLoaded(workerId: string): Promise<void> {
312	    const state = this.getState(workerId);
313	    if (state.status === "loading") {
314	      return this.inFlightByWorker.get(workerId) ?? Promise.resolve();
315	    }
316	    const loadedEmptyWithoutProof = state.status === "loaded"
317	      && state.latestContiguousSeq === 0
318	      && state.latestKnownSeq === 0
319	      && state.entries.length === 0;
320	    if (
321	      state.status === "loaded"

323	      && !loadedEmptyWithoutProof
324	      && !state.needsTailValidation
325	    ) {
326	      return Promise.resolve();
327	    }
328	    // Cold start (no entries yet) → tail-load the last N entries instead

331	      return this.fetchTail(workerId, DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT);
332	    }
333	    return this.fetchForward(workerId, state.latestContiguousSeq);
334	  }

336	  /**

340	   */
341	  loadOlder(workerId: string, limit: number = DEFAULT_OLDER_LIMIT): Promise<void> {
342	    const existing = this.inFlightByWorker.get(workerId);

344	      if (this.olderLoadInFlightByWorker.has(workerId)) {

348	      // reaches the top of the viewport. Preserve that scroll-back intent and
349	      // start the older-page request after the refresh settles.

351	        () => this.loadOlder(workerId, limit),

362	    });

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-29T18:17:44.016Z

The subscribe/unsubscribe pattern gives me the signal for background pruning: when a worker's listener set is empty, it's not rendered, so I can collapse it to the hot window. Now I'm planning the files needed—a new retention module with policy constants and a pruning function, plus updates to the entries manager to prune after forward merges.

For the transcript oldestToken problem, I want a shared codec module with platform-neutral base64url encoding so both the manager and route use the same logic. I'm starting with the retention module now, defining a policy object with hot window size, max retained entries, and scrollback idle timeout, then writing a function to compute how many entries should actually be retained based on visibility and staleness.

The retained count logic keeps just the hot window when the terminal isn't visible or scrollback is stale, otherwise keeps up to the max, clamped between the two bounds. I'll pair this with a pruning helper that returns the same array reference when nothing needs trimming, avoiding unnecessary re-renders. No user-facing strings here so no i18n concerns—writing the file now.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/entry-retention.ts",
  "content": "/**\n * Retention policy for in-memory transcript windows.\n *\n * Both `WorkerEntriesManager` and `ConversationTranscriptManager` grow their\n * `entries` array on every forward fetch. A session that streams output for a\n * day accumulates tens of thousands of entries, all of which stay mounted in\n * the DOM and get rebuilt into activity items on every append. The tail limit\n * applied at hydration time does not help, because it is only consulted on\n * mount.\n *\n * The window is therefore garbage collected as it grows:\n *   - The newest `hotWindow` entries are always retained.\n *   - Scrolling back (a `loadOlder` call) marks the stream as recently\n *     accessed and raises the ceiling to `maxRetained` so that reading through\n *     history does not fight a collector that keeps dropping what was just\n *     fetched.\n *   - Once `scrollbackIdleMs` elapses with no further scrollback, the window\n *     collapses to `hotWindow` again. Live output arriving at the bottom is\n *     deliberately not an access signal: a session left streaming all day must\n *     still prune.\n *   - A stream with no subscribers is not on screen, so it collapses to\n *     `hotWindow` immediately rather than waiting out the idle timer.\n *\n * Trimming the head always implies `hasOlder`, since the dropped entries are\n * still on the server and scroll-back must be able to fetch them again.\n */\n\nexport interface EntryRetentionPolicy {\n  /** Entries always kept, regardless of age or access. */\n  hotWindow: number;\n  /** Ceiling while scrollback is being actively used. */\n  maxRetained: number;\n  /** How long a scrollback keeps the ceiling raised. */\n  scrollbackIdleMs: number;\n}\n\nexport const DEFAULT_ENTRY_RETENTION: EntryRetentionPolicy = {\n  hotWindow: 300,\n  maxRetained: 2000,\n  scrollbackIdleMs: 10 * 60 * 1000,\n};\n\nexport interface RetainedCountInput {\n  total: number;\n  /** Timestamp of the last scroll-back load, or null if never. */\n  lastScrollbackAt: number | null;\n  now: number;\n  /** False when nothing is rendering this stream. */\n  isVisible: boolean;\n  policy?: EntryRetentionPolicy;\n}\n\n/**\n * How many trailing entries should stay in memory. Never returns more than\n * `total`, so callers can compare against the current length to decide whether\n * a trim is needed at all.\n */\nexport function retainedEntryCount(input: RetainedCountInput): number {\n  const policy = input.policy ?? DEFAULT_ENTRY_RETENTION;\n  const ceiling = Math.max(policy.hotWindow, policy.maxRetained);\n  const scrollbackIsRecent = input.isVisible\n    && input.lastScrollbackAt !== null\n    && input.now - input.lastScrollbackAt <= policy.scrollbackIdleMs;\n  const allowance = scrollbackIsRecent ? ceiling : policy.hotWindow;\n  return Math.min(input.total, allowance);\n}\n\n/**\n * Drop entries from the head so that at most `keep` remain. Returns the\n * original array reference when nothing is dropped, so that callers preserve\n * identity for memoized consumers.\n */\nexport function pruneEntryWindow<T>(entries: T[], keep: number): T[] {\n  if (keep >= entries.length) {\n    return entries;\n  }\n  return entries.slice(entries.length - Math.max(0, keep));\n}\n"
}

I'll add a `retention` field with a test override, then apply the edits.

  "new_string": "  private markWake(workerId: string): void {\n    this.wakeVersionByWorker.set(workerId, (this.wakeVersionByWorker.get(workerId) ?? 0) + 1);\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Garbage collect the head of a worker's window. See `entry-retention.ts`\n   * for the policy. Returns the original array when nothing is dropped so\n   * memoized consumers keep their identity.\n   */\n  private retainedEntriesFor(workerId: string, entries: WorkerEntry[]): WorkerEntry[] {\n    const keep = retainedEntryCount({\n      total: entries.length,\n      lastScrollbackAt: this.lastScrollbackAtByWorker.get(workerId) ?? null,\n      now: this.now(),\n      isVisible: (this.listenersByWorker.get(workerId)?.size ?? 0) > 0,\n      policy: this.retention,\n    });\n    return pruneEntryWindow(entries, keep);\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Collapse a worker's window to the hot tail. Called when the last\n   * subscriber goes away: an unrendered stream has no reason to hold\n   * scroll-back in memory, and re-entering the conversation refetches.\n   */\n  private collectUnsubscribedWorker(workerId: string): void {\n    const state = this.stateByWorker.get(workerId);\n    if (!state || state.entries.length === 0) {\n      return;\n    }\n    const retained = this.retainedEntriesFor(workerId, state.entries);\n    if (retained.length === state.entries.length) {\n      return;\n    }\n    this.stateByWorker.set(workerId, {\n      ...state,\n      entries: retained,\n      lowestSeq: retained[0]?.seq ?? state.lowestSeq,\n      hasOlder: true,\n    });\n  }"
}

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-29T18:19:12.467Z

The contiguous window invariant still holds after trimming, but I'm realizing there's a risk: if retention caps entries at 300 while a user is scrolled up reading older messages, trimming could yank content out from under them.

Actually there's a worse problem — trimming while scrolled up causes a scroll jump since visible rows disappear. Even with the 2000-entry ceiling for recent scrollback, it's still possible. A cleaner fix would be to only trim when the view is following the latest messages, which means adding a way for the Terminal to tell the manager whether it's currently at the bottom.

I'm weighing whether that's worth the added scope, since it's per-Terminal state feeding into a global manager. I'll keep the simpler scrollback-recency approach for now but flag the scroll-jump edge case as a known limitation rather than silently accepting the bug.

Turning to the ConversationTranscriptManager, I need to handle the oldestToken problem when trimming the transcript head — the token is a base64url-encoded cursor map, and trimming must update it to point at the new head correctly. I'm going to check the route's token semantics around lines 165-215 to confirm how beforeToken comparisons work.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-29T18:19:35.287Z

I've confirmed the cursor semantics: the token's seq value is an exclusive upper bound representing the earliest retained seq. So after trimming, the client should encode a new oldest token using the earliest retained seq per worker, but for workers with no retained entries, the previous cursor needs to be preserved rather than dropped.

That means I need a shared codec for encoding/decoding the token, since the route currently can't decode it without one. I'll create a platform-neutral base64url encode/decode utility that works in both browser and Node, then update the trim logic in ConversationTranscriptManager to decode the existing token, overwrite cursors only for workers with retained entries, and leave the rest untouched before re-encoding.

A cleaner invariant: for workers with retained entries, cursor is their lowest retained seq; for workers with none retained, cursor is one past their highest trimmed seq, so loadOlder still fetches them correctly. That's workable, but it's enough fiddly bookkeeping that I should pause and ask whether the transcript manager path is really where the actual problem lies.

The user's session is direct-control and long-running, which means Terminal likely goes through `usingUnifiedStream` rather than ConversationTranscriptManager. I should check Terminal.tsx around line 2517 to see how that flag gets decided and where the transcript manager entries actually feed in — if the unified stream path is what's active here, fixing transcript trimming alone won't help, and I'd need to scope it separately from the WorkerEntriesManager and memo identity fix the user already approved.

    unifiedWorkerStreamEnabled ? selectedRunId : null,

      enabled: unifiedWorkerStreamEnabled && Boolean(selectedRunId),
      refreshIntervalMs: resolveDirectWorkerStreamRefreshInterval({
        unifiedWorkerStreamEnabled,
        primaryConversationWorkerId,
        activeRefreshIntervalMs: DIRECT_WORKER_STREAM_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MS,
        validationIntervalMs: DIRECT_WORKER_STREAM_VALIDATION_INTERVAL_MS,
        showDirectControlWorkingIndicator,
        selectedRunIsTerminal,
      }),
    },
  );
  // The run's worker list, not the entries loaded so far, decides how the
  // conversation is ordered — otherwise the rule flips from seq to timestamp
  // the moment the first transcript page arrives and the list re-sorts under
  // the reader. Undefined until the transcript answers, which leaves the

  const conversationRanOnMultipleWorkers = conversationTranscript.workerIds.length > 0
    ? conversationTranscript.workerIds.length > 1
    : undefined;

  // tiny (usually empty), so round-tripping it is far cheaper than the rebuild

  const supersededSeqRangesKey = JSON.stringify(primaryConversationAgent?.supersededSeqRanges ?? []);

    () => JSON.parse(supersededSeqRangesKey) as SupersededSeqRange[],
    [supersededSeqRangesKey],

  // whole timeline *inside* the timeline map — O(n^2) with two `Date`
  // allocations per candidate, so a 500-item supervisor conversation cost
  // ~250k iterations and ~500k Date parses on every render.
  const latestClarificationAnswerMsByRunId = useMemo(() => {
    const latest = new Map<string, number>();
    for (const candidate of conversationTimelineItems) {
      if (candidate.type !== "message" || candidate.message.kind !== "clarification_answer") {
        continue;
      }
      const candidateMs = new Date(candidate.message.createdAt).getTime();
      const existing = latest.get(candidate.message.runId);
      if (existing === undefined || candidateMs > existing) {
        latest.set(candidate.message.runId, candidateMs);
      }
    }
    return latest;
  }, [conversationTimelineItems]);

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-29T18:20:02.788Z

Since the merged transcript takes priority over the worker stream, ConversationTranscriptManager is likely the array feeding Terminal for the day-long session, so I need to cap that path too. I'm setting up a shared token module with functions to decode and encode transcript cursor tokens using cross-platform base64url, then wiring the route to use them.

I'll need to keep the existing exported names for tests while re-exporting through the shared codec, and update fetchForward to prune entries and recompute the oldest token. For the base64url encoding, I need to feature-detect between Buffer (Node) and btoa (browser) since this code runs on both server and web interface.

Writing base64url encoding logic...

For decoding, I'm mirroring the same feature-detection: use Buffer when available, otherwise fall back to atob and TextDecoder for browser environments, being careful that unpadded base64 still decodes correctly.

Actually, I'm reconsidering whether client-side decoding is even necessary. Thinking through the trim logic, cursors need to be computed differently for workers with retained entries, workers fully trimmed, and workers that were never loaded into memory at all — and I'm realizing that a worker with zero returned entries gets no cursor set, which means it's treated as absent and defaults to not being fetched at all, so that "never in memory" case might not actually need decoding after all.

But the tricky part is a worker whose entries were entirely trimmed in an earlier trim pass — it's neither retained now nor part of the current trimmed batch, so its cursor from the previous token has to be preserved somehow, which does seem to require decoding unless I track cursors separately in state.

Decoding via a shared codec is the pragmatic path here. Now I'll write both files.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/shared/conversation-transcript-token.ts",
  "content": "/**\n * Codec for the conversation transcript pagination cursor.\n *\n * `seq` is per-worker, so a merged transcript cannot page on a single\n * number. The token carries one cursor per worker, base64url-encoded JSON.\n *\n * Semantics:\n *   - As `afterToken`, `cursors[workerId]` is the highest seq the client has\n *     consumed from that worker; the server returns entries strictly newer.\n *   - As `beforeToken`, `cursors[workerId]` is the lowest seq the client\n *     holds; the server returns entries strictly older. A worker absent from\n *     the map is treated as 0, which yields nothing.\n *\n * This lives in `shared/` because the frontend also has to mint a token: when\n * the retention collector drops the head of an in-memory transcript, the\n * server's `oldestToken` no longer describes the client's window, and\n * scroll-back would skip the entries that were just dropped.\n */\n\nexport interface ConversationTranscriptToken {\n  cursors: Record<string, number>;\n}\n\nfunction encodeUtf8ToBase64Url(text: string): string {\n  const base64 = typeof Buffer !== \"undefined\"\n    ? Buffer.from(text, \"utf8\").toString(\"base64\")\n    : btoa(String.fromCharCode(...new TextEncoder().encode(text)));\n  return base64.replace(/\\+/g, \"-\").replace(/\\//g, \"_\").replace(/=+$/, \"\");\n}\n\nfunction decodeBase64UrlToUtf8(raw: string): string {\n  const base64 = raw.replace(/-/g, \"+\").replace(/_/g, \"/\");\n  if (typeof Buffer !== \"undefined\") {\n    return Buffer.from(base64, \"base64\").toString(\"utf8\");\n  }\n  const padded = base64.padEnd(Math.ceil(base64.length / 4) * 4, \"=\");\n  const binary = atob(padded);\n  return new TextDecoder().decode(Uint8Array.from(binary, (char) => char.charCodeAt(0)));\n}\n\n/** Malformed or absent tokens decode to an empty cursor map (cold start). */\nexport function decodeConversationTranscriptToken(raw: string | null | undefined): ConversationTranscriptToken {\n  if (!raw) {\n    return { cursors: {} };\n  }\n  try {\n    const parsed = JSON.parse(decodeBase64UrlToUtf8(raw)) as ConversationTranscriptToken;\n    if (parsed && typeof parsed === \"object\" && parsed.cursors && typeof parsed.cursors === \"object\") {\n      const cursors: Record<string, number> = {};\n      for (const [workerId, value] of Object.entries(parsed.cursors)) {\n        if (typeof workerId === \"string\" && typeof value === \"number\" && Number.isFinite(value) && value >= 0) {\n          cursors[workerId] = value;\n        }\n      }\n      return { cursors };\n    }\n  } catch {\n    // Malformed token → treat as cold start.\n  }\n  return { cursors: {} };\n}\n\nexport function encodeConversationTranscriptToken(token: [REDACTED] string {\n  return encodeUtf8ToBase64Url(JSON.stringify(token));\n}\n"
}

  "new_string": "  /**\n   * Garbage collect the head of a run's merged transcript. See\n   * `entry-retention.ts` for the policy.\n   *\n   * Dropping entries invalidates the server's `oldestToken`, which describes\n   * the window the server last handed us rather than the one we still hold —\n   * paging back from it would skip everything we just dropped. A replacement\n   * token is minted from the retained entries: each worker still in the\n   * window pages back from its lowest retained seq, and a worker whose\n   * entries were dropped entirely pages back from just above its highest\n   * dropped seq so those entries are reachable again.\n   */\n  private applyRetention(\n    runId: string,\n    entries: ConversationTranscriptEntry[],\n    oldestToken: string | null,\n    hasOlder: boolean,\n  ): Pick<ConversationTranscriptState, \"entries\" | \"oldestToken\" | \"hasOlder\"> {\n    const keep = retainedEntryCount({\n      total: entries.length,\n      lastScrollbackAt: this.lastScrollbackAtByRunId.get(runId) ?? null,\n      now: this.now(),\n      isVisible: (this.listenersByRunId.get(runId)?.size ?? 0) > 0,\n      policy: this.retention,\n    });\n    const retained = pruneEntryWindow(entries, keep);\n    if (retained.length === entries.length) {\n      return { entries, oldestToken, hasOlder };\n    }\n\n    const dropped = entries.slice(0, entries.length - retained.length);\n    const cursors = { ...decodeConversationTranscriptToken(oldestToken).cursors };\n    const highestDropped = new Map<string, number>();\n    for (const entry of dropped) {\n      const seq = Math.floor(entry.seq);\n      const existing = highestDropped.get(entry.workerId);\n      if (existing === undefined || seq > existing) {\n        highestDropped.set(entry.workerId, seq);\n      }\n    }\n    const lowestRetained = new Map<string, number>();\n    for (const entry of retained) {\n      const seq = Math.floor(entry.seq);\n      const existing = lowestRetained.get(entry.workerId);\n      if (existing === undefined || seq < existing) {\n        lowestRetained.set(entry.workerId, seq);\n      }\n    }\n    for (const [workerId, seq] of highestDropped) {\n      cursors[workerId] = seq + 1;\n    }\n    // A worker still present in the window overrides the dropped-cursor\n    // above: its lowest retained seq is the real lower bound.\n    for (const [workerId, seq] of lowestRetained) {\n      cursors[workerId] = seq;\n    }\n\n    return {\n      entries: retained,\n      oldestToken: encodeConversationTranscriptToken({ cursors }),\n      hasOlder: true,\n    };\n  }\n\n  private updateState(runId: string, next: ConversationTranscriptState): void {"

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-29T18:21:47.598Z

I'm designing a generic reconcile function for structural equality: reference-equal returns true immediately, arrays compare length and elements, plain objects compare keys and values, and everything else falls back to Object.is. Since entry objects tend to stay stable across rebuilds, the reference-equality shortcut should handle `raw` fields correctly in the common case, though a deep compare would still recurse into `raw` if references actually differ.

To bound the cost of deep comparison, I'll cap recursion depth and fall back to reference comparison beyond that — false negatives are acceptable since they only cost performance, not correctness, since safe object reuse depends solely on rendering content matching. For tool activities and tool groups, since we rebuild fresh objects each cycle, deep equal will correctly detect unchanged content and let us reuse the previous reference. I'm keying entries by `id` since that's what the row component uses for its React key.

I'm writing a reconciliation function that maps previous items by id, then for each new item checks if a matching previous item exists with the same kind and deep-equal content — if so it reuses the old reference to preserve identity, otherwise it keeps the new object; it returns the original array unchanged if nothing was reused.

Now I need to find where the useMemo in Terminal produces the final activity array, near the end of that block, and wire this reconciliation in there.

  "old_string": "export function buildAgentOutputActivity(snapshot: AgentOutputSnapshot): AgentActivityItem[] {",
  "new_string": "/**\n * Structural comparison used to decide whether a rebuilt activity item is\n * indistinguishable from the one already on screen.\n *\n * Reference equality short-circuits, which covers the expensive case: a\n * protocol item's `raw` payload is the entry's own object, and entries are\n * stable across rebuilds. Anything past `maxDepth` compares by reference,\n * so an unusually deep payload costs a missed reuse rather than a long walk.\n */\nfunction activityValuesEqual(a: unknown, b: unknown, maxDepth = 6): boolean {\n  if (Object.is(a, b)) {\n    return true;\n  }\n  if (maxDepth <= 0 || typeof a !== \"object\" || typeof b !== \"object\" || a === null || b === null) {\n    return false;\n  }\n  if (Array.isArray(a) || Array.isArray(b)) {\n    if (!Array.isArray(a) || !Array.isArray(b) || a.length !== b.length) {\n      return false;\n    }\n    return a.every((value, index) => activityValuesEqual(value, b[index], maxDepth - 1));\n  }\n  const aRecord = a as Record<string, unknown>;\n  const bRecord = b as Record<string, unknown>;\n  const aKeys = Object.keys(aRecord);\n  const bKeys = Object.keys(bRecord);\n  if (aKeys.length !== bKeys.length) {\n    return false;\n  }\n  return aKeys.every((key) => (\n    Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(bRecord, key)\n    && activityValuesEqual(aRecord[key], bRecord[key], maxDepth - 1)\n  ));\n}\n\n/**\n * Restore object identity for activity items whose content did not change.\n *\n * `buildAgentOutputActivity` reconstructs every item from scratch on each\n * call, so a single appended entry hands the renderer a brand-new object for\n * every row in the transcript. `ActivityRow` is memoized on those objects, so\n * its shallow comparison failed for all of them and the whole transcript\n * re-rendered — including markdown and diff parsing — several times a second\n * on a streaming session.\n *\n * Substituting the previous reference wherever the content is unchanged makes\n * that memo effective again: only rows that actually changed re-render.\n */\nexport function reconcileActivityIdentity(\n  previous: AgentActivityItem[] | null | undefined,\n  next: AgentActivityItem[],\n): AgentActivityItem[] {\n  if (!previous || previous.length === 0 || next.length === 0) {\n    return next;\n  }\n  const previousById = new Map<string, AgentActivityItem>();\n  for (const item of previous) {\n    previousById.set(item.id, item);\n  }\n  let reusedAny = false;\n  const reconciled = next.map((item) => {\n    const candidate = previousById.get(item.id);\n    if (candidate && candidate.kind === item.kind && activityValuesEqual(candidate, item)) {\n      reusedAny = true;\n      return candidate;\n    }\n    return item;\n  });\n  return reusedAny ? reconciled : next;\n}\n\nexport function buildAgentOutputActivity(snapshot: AgentOutputSnapshot): AgentActivityItem[] {"
}

import { describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import type { WorkerEntry } from "@/server/workers/entries-types";
import { EMPTY_WORKER_STREAM_STATE, WorkerEntriesManager, coalesceWorkerEntriesById } from "@/interface/home/WorkerEntriesManager";

describe("coalesceWorkerEntriesById", () => {
  function asst(seq: number, text: string, id = "msg", timestamp = "2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z"): WorkerEntry {
    return { id, seq, type: "message", text, timestamp, authorRole: "assistant", channel: "agent" } as WorkerEntry;
  }
  function user(seq: number, text: string, id = `user-${seq}`, timestamp = "2026-01-01T00:00:05.000Z"): WorkerEntry {
    return { id, seq, type: "user_input", text, timestamp, authorRole: "user", channel: "stdin" } as WorkerEntry;
  }

  it("keeps a single row per id at its first appearance, with the latest text", () => {
    const out = coalesceWorkerEntriesById([
      asst(1, "Hello"),
      user(2, "your message"),
      asst(3, "Hello world"),
    ]);
    expect(out.map((e) => ({ id: e.id, seq: e.seq, text: e.text }))).toEqual([
      { id: "msg", seq: 3, text: "Hello world" },
      { id: "user-2", seq: 2, text: "your message" },
    ]);
  });

  it("places a re-delivered message where the newest worker has it", () => {
    // A rewind re-delivers the message on a new worker with a new timestamp.
    // That copy is the live one; the older worker's copy belongs to the branch
    // the user discarded.
    const out = coalesceWorkerEntriesById([
      { ...user(695, "the question", "edited", "2026-07-25T09:36:34.136Z"), workerId: "run-worker-1" } as WorkerEntry,
      { ...user(2, "the question", "edited", "2026-07-25T10:06:43.720Z"), workerId: "run-worker-2" } as WorkerEntry,
    ], ["run-worker-1", "run-worker-2"]);
    expect(out).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(out[0]).toMatchObject({ id: "edited", timestamp: "2026-07-25T10:06:43.720Z" });
  });

  it("keeps history backfilled onto a newer worker at its original send time", () => {
    // `reconcileWorkerUserMessagesInStream` replays old messages onto a fresh
    // worker using the original createdAt. The opening prompt must stay at the
    // top of the conversation, not move to whenever the replay ran.
    const out = coalesceWorkerEntriesById([
      { ...user(1, "opening prompt", "first", "2026-05-24T12:06:59.692Z"), workerId: "run-worker-1" } as WorkerEntry,
      { ...user(220, "opening prompt", "first", "2026-05-24T15:28:06.421Z"), workerId: "run-worker-2" } as WorkerEntry,
      { ...user(25, "opening prompt", "first", "2026-05-24T12:06:59.000Z"), workerId: "run-worker-3" } as WorkerEntry,
    ], ["run-worker-1", "run-worker-2", "run-worker-3"]);
    expect(out).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(out[0]).toMatchObject({ id: "first", timestamp: "2026-05-24T12:06:59.000Z" });
  });

  it("keeps the earliest timestamp when there is no worker order to judge by", () => {
    const out = coalesceWorkerEntriesById([
      user(695, "the question", "edited", "2026-07-25T09:36:34.136Z"),
      user(2, "the question", "edited", "2026-07-25T10:06:43.720Z"),
    ]);
    expect(out[0]).toMatchObject({ id: "edited", timestamp: "2026-07-25T09:36:34.136Z" });
  });

  it("preserves entries without an id verbatim", () => {
    const noId = { seq: 5, type: "message", text: "synthetic" } as WorkerEntry;
    const out = coalesceWorkerEntriesById([asst(1, "first"), noId, asst(2, "second")]);

import { describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import type { WorkerEntry } from "@/server/workers/entries-types";
import { ConversationTranscriptManager } from "@/interface/home/ConversationTranscriptManager";

function token(label: string) {
  return Buffer.from(JSON.stringify({ label }), "utf8").toString("base64url");
}

function entry(seq: number, workerId = "w1"): WorkerEntry & { workerId: string } {
  return {
    id: `${workerId}-entry-${seq}`,
    workerId,
    seq,
    type: "message",
    text: `message ${seq}`,
    timestamp: new Date(1700000000000 + seq).toISOString(),
  } as WorkerEntry & { workerId: string };
}

describe("ConversationTranscriptManager", () => {
  it("tail-loads the selected run and prepends older pages on scroll-back", async () => {
    const tailToken = token("tail");
    const oldestToken = token("oldest");
    const olderOldestToken = token("older-oldest");
    const transcript = vi
      .fn()
      .mockResolvedValueOnce({
        entries: [entry(152), entry(153)],
        latestToken: tailToken,
        oldestToken,
        hasOlder: true,
        workerIds: ["w1"],
      })
      .mockResolvedValueOnce({
        entries: [entry(150), entry(151)],
        latestToken: tailToken,
        oldestToken: olderOldestToken,
        hasOlder: true,
        workerIds: ["w1"],
      });
    const manager = new ConversationTranscriptManager({
      transcript,
    });

    await manager.ensureLoaded("run-1");

function buildManager(responses: Array<{ entries: WorkerEntry[]; latestSeq: number }>) {
  let call = 0;
  const listEntries = vi.fn(async () => {
    const response = responses[call] ?? responses[responses.length - 1];
    call += 1;
    return response;
  });
  const manager = new WorkerEntriesManager({ listEntries });
  return { manager, requestJson: listEntries };
}

function memoryStorage(): Pick<Storage, "getItem" | "setItem" | "removeItem"> {
  const values = new Map<string, string>();
  return {
    getItem: (key) => values.get(key) ?? null,
    setItem: (key, value) => {
      values.set(key, value);
    },
    removeItem: (key) => {
      values.delete(key);
    },
  };
}

});

  private fetchBefore(workerId: string, beforeSeq: number, limit: number): Promise<void> {
    const existing = this.inFlightByWorker.get(workerId);
    if (existing) return existing;
    const previous = this.getState(workerId);
    this.updateState(workerId, { ...previous, status: "loading", lastError: null });
    const promise = this.load({ workerId, beforeSeq, limit }).then(
      (response) => {
        const current = this.getState(workerId);
        // Only accept entries strictly older than our current window and
        // adjacent to it (no gap to lowestSeq). If we'd introduce a gap,
        // drop the page — `loadOlder` can be retried.
        const sorted = [...response.entries]
          .filter((entry) => typeof entry.seq === "number" && entry.seq > 0 && entry.seq < current.lowestSeq)
          .sort((a, b) => a.seq - b.seq);
        if (sorted.length === 0) {
          this.updateState(workerId, {
            ...current,
            hasOlder: response.hasOlder ?? false,
            status: "loaded",
            lastError: null,
          });
          return;
        }
        const highestOlderSeq = sorted[sorted.length - 1]!.seq;
        if (current.lowestSeq > 0 && highestOlderSeq !== current.lowestSeq - 1) {
          // Gap — older page doesn't butt up to our window. Don't merge
          // (would violate the contiguity invariant). Still advance the
          // hasOlder hint if the server thinks there's nothing left.
          this.updateState(workerId, {
            ...current,
            hasOlder: response.hasOlder ?? current.hasOlder,
            status: "loaded",
            lastError: null,
          });
          return;
        }
        const lowest = sorted[0]!.seq;
        const merged = [...sorted, ...current.entries];
        this.updateState(workerId, {
          ...current,
          entries: merged,
          lowestSeq: lowest,
          hasOlder: response.hasOlder ?? (lowest > 1),
          status: "loaded",
          lastError: null,
        });
      },
      (error: unknown) => {
        const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);
        const failing = this.getState(workerId);

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-29T18:27:31.234Z

The build succeeds, and I confirm the Buffer reference in the shared token module uses a safe typeof guard that won't break in the browser bundle, even if a Buffer polyfill gets injected. I want to verify the browser bundle actually includes this token module without any risky Buffer usage.

Now I'm reviewing the diff for correctness. A genuine edge case concerns me: if a user scrolls up into history for over 10 minutes while streaming continues, the age-based garbage collection could suddenly collapse content above them and jump their scroll position when they're not actively following the latest messages. This is an accepted trade-off given the design, but I could cheaply mitigate it by preventing collapse below the current window while the view isn't following the latest — that would need a signal from the Terminal component, so I'm checking whether an existing hook already provides this.

I find that `shouldFollowLatestRef` only exists for the "terminal" variant, while ConversationMain uses the "native" variant where the outer page scrolls instead. Given that 300 rows of hot window is substantial, this scenario requires scrolling far up and idling for 10+ minutes — plausible but uncommon, so I'll leave it as-is and flag it as a known limitation with a possible follow-up rather than fixing it now.

I'm now double-checking the `applyFetchResult` logic for computing `nextLowest`, comparing my trimmed-aware version against the original to confirm they produce the same result when `previous.lowestSeq` is zero and entries are empty — both correctly fall back to zero, so that case checks out.

I also verify that `hasOlder` correctly reflects trimming state, and that `collectUnsubscribedWorker` sets `hasOlder: true` whenever it trims, which is right since there are no active listeners to notify at that point — the mutation is safe because `useSyncExternalStore` will just pick up the new state whenever the component resubscribes. I'm satisfied this doesn't violate the snapshot-stability requirement, since the change happens during cleanup when nothing is subscribed.

There's a minor React 18 StrictMode wrinkle: dev-mode mount→unmount→remount would collapse and then re-fetch the window, causing an extra fetch in development, but that's acceptable. The bigger tradeoff is that rapid unmount/remount cycles (tab switching, re-render churn) will drop scrollback each time — but that's the intended behavior per the "prune background streams to hot window" design. I confirm that `applyRetention` inside `collectUnsubscribedRun` correctly treats visibility as false once listeners are removed, so it falls back to the hot window as expected.

For `WorkerEntriesManager.collectUnsubscribedWorker`, the same logic holds. Now I want to go back through the actual diff to catch anything I might have missed.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-29T18:27:50.615Z

The diff checks out. I'm noting that DEFAULT_TAIL_LIMIT of 100 is now smaller than hotWindow=300, which isn't a bug, just a smaller initial window, so I'll leave it. I also confirm fetchTail's response limit of 100 stays under hotWindow, so no trimming is needed there, and now I'm looking at what fetchBefore does.

fetchBefore intentionally omits retention since it handles active scrollback, and applyRetention forces hasOlder true when entries get trimmed, which is correct behavior. There's a subtler risk though: coalesceWorkerEntriesById merges current and incoming entries by keeping the first appearance of an id, so if the server later re-sends a revision of an entry that was already trimmed from memory, it would now surface as a brand-new row at the bottom rather than updating its original position — previously this wasn't an issue since all entries stayed resident. I'm weighing whether this scenario is actually plausible, since revisions typically happen while a message is still live and being streamed.

Considering `reconcileWorkerUserMessagesInStream`, which backfills history onto a freshly spawned worker and re-emits old user_input ids — if those ids were already trimmed, they'd reappear at the bottom instead of coalescing into their original spot, causing visible duplication. This mainly happens right after a worker respawn when the window is still small, so the risk is narrow given a hotWindow of 300. I considered adding a trimmed-ids guard to suppress these, but that could wrongly hide a legitimately redelivered message, so I'll just flag this as a known tradeoff rather than fix it now.

I've already run tests/app, tests/lib, and tests/shared (all passing), plus tests/api, tests/ui, and tests/interface (3 pre-existing failures unrelated to my change), typecheck is clean, and build is clean. Since my change touches a server-side route for the token codec, I should also run tests/server to be safe, and check whether there's a lint script to run as well.

## Claude thinking · 2026-08-29T18:28:32.604Z

Everything looks complete now. I'll summarize the changes: a new entry-retention policy module, pruning and scrollback clock logic added to both WorkerEntriesManager and ConversationTranscriptManager along with oldestToken minting, a new shared conversation transcript token codec used by the route, and a reconcileActivityIdentity addition to agent-output.ts.

I also added an identity cache ref in Terminal.tsx and four new test files. I verified typecheck is clean, all new tests pass along with the existing 733, and the build succeeds — the 4 pre-existing failures were confirmed unrelated by stashing. I want to flag a few known limitations: a scroll-jump issue when parked in scrollback during a long streaming session, no virtualization for the row count, and re-delivered backfilled entries appearing at the bottom instead of coalescing. I should also mention I didn't verify this in the live running app since the symptom takes a day to manifest.

## Claude · 2026-08-29T18:28:43.925Z

Done. Both halves are in, typecheck and build are clean.

**Root cause fixed — the window is now bounded.** New `src/interface/home/entry-retention.ts` holds the policy: newest 300 entries always kept, ceiling of 2000 while scrollback was used in the last 10 minutes, collapse back to 300 otherwise. Only a `loadOlder` resets the clock, so a session streaming at the bottom all day prunes itself.

Wired into both growth paths:
- `WorkerEntriesManager.ts:680-707` — the forward merge now prunes the head, moves `lowestSeq` to the new head, and sets `hasOlder` so scroll-back can refetch.
- `ConversationTranscriptManager.ts:301-336` — same, and this is the one that mattered most: `ConversationMain.tsx:910` prefers the merged transcript, so it was the array actually feeding your session.

Trimming the transcript head invalidates the server's `oldestToken`, which describes the window the server sent rather than the one still held — paging back from it would skip everything just dropped. So `applyRetention` mints a replacement cursor, and the codec moved to `src/shared/conversation-transcript-token.ts` with the route importing it instead of keeping its own copy.

Both managers also collapse to the hot window when their last subscriber unsubscribes.

**Re-render storm fixed.** `reconcileActivityIdentity` in `agent-output.ts:1259` hands back the previous object for any row whose content is unchanged. `buildAgentOutputActivity` allocates fresh objects for every row on every call, which is why the `memo` on `ActivityRow` — and the comment at `Terminal.tsx:2190` claiming the shallow compare was meaningful — never held. Terminal keeps the identity cache in a ref and applies it at the end of the activity memo.

**Verification:** typecheck clean; `pnpm build:interface:web` succeeds; 4 new test files (27 tests) pass; `tests/app` + `tests/lib` + `tests/shared` all 733 pass. Four failures exist elsewhere (`route-contract-fixture`, `next-standalone-route-parity`, `events-route`, `terminal-fit`, `supervisor-ordering-source`) — I confirmed each reproduces with my changes stashed, so they belong to the account-auth work already in your tree. I have not watched a real day-long session, so the fix is verified by tests and reasoning, not by reproducing your symptom.

Three things worth knowing:

The DOM is bounded now but still unvirtualized — 300 rows, not 20,000. If that's still choppy, virtualization is the remaining lever.

If you park in scrollback for more than 10 minutes on a session that keeps streaming, the next append collapses the window and content above you disappears, jumping the scroll. Closing that properly means feeding a "following latest" signal from Terminal into the managers; say the word and I'll add it.

An old entry that gets re-delivered after being trimmed (history backfill onto a fresh worker after a respawn) will land at the bottom as a new row rather than coalescing back into its original position. Narrow, since backfill happens right after a respawn when the window is still small.
